replace 2D Gum-Net: Unsupervised Dense Elastic Image Registration via Symmetric Correlation and Spectral Pooling

Authors: Aarya Bhave, Anushka Ajay Bhave

Abstract: Dense non-rigid registration, the recovery of a pixel-wise deformation field that warps one image into geometric agreement with another, is fundamental across medical imaging, structural biology, and correspondence estimation, yet learning-based approaches face a severe bottleneck: ground-truth deformation fields are notoriously difficult to obtain for real data. We present 2D Gum-Net, an end-to-end architecture that predicts dense elastic deformation fields fully unsupervised. We transfer the Geometric Unsupervised Matching Network (Gum-Net), originally developed for 3D subtomogram alignment in cryo-electron tomography, to 2D elastic registration: all three modules are redesigned in 2D, and the rigid transformation head is replaced by a Dense Spatial Transformer that regresses a sparse control-point grid, lifted to a full-resolution field $\Phi$ by bicubic interpolation. Downsampling uses DCT spectral pooling, implemented as frozen-basis matrix multiplications ($\approx 50\times$ GPU speedup), preserving the fine, quasi-periodic structure that max and average pooling destroy. We evaluate on contact-based fingerprints, a challenging testbed for this task: locally self-similar ridge patterns make correspondence maximally ambiguous, skin pressed against a sensor generates authentic large-displacement elastic distortion, and minutiae act as precise landmarks through which an independent matcher externally verifies the topological correctness of predicted fields. Trained purely on synthetic impressions and evaluated zero-shot on real benchmarks, our method improves structural correlation by up to 15.7% on FVC2004 and raises genuine match scores under an independent matcher while leaving imposter scores essentially unchanged. This suggests that the predicted deformations restore true structure without fabricating correspondences.

replace AcrossPlace: Do Vision--Language Models Recognize Places Beyond Their Source Views? A Comprehensive Benchmark

Authors: Zhenyu Li, Tianyi Shang, Shuhan Dong, Ruotong Xu, Qingyu Zhu

Abstract: Language-guided place recognition requires recovering geographic identity across visual observations beyond the image from which a description originates. However, aggregate retrieval scores often entangle observation transfer, gallery competition, and linguistic evidence, obscuring the underlying recognition capability. We introduce AcrossPlace, a provenance-aware benchmark establishing a systematic evaluation standard for bidirectional language--vision place recognition. Built upon GSV-Cities, AcrossPlace comprises 56,726 places across 23 cities, 156,800 automatically generated, rule-filtered descriptions, and 113,404 target images. Each description retains its source-image provenance, while retrieval operates on disjoint target observations. Text-to-image (T2I) and image-to-text (I2T) retrieval share a unified place-identity relevance criterion supporting multiple valid matches. Two geographic protocols evaluate unseen places within represented cities (P0) and generalization to held-out cities (P1). Paired observation comparisons, gallery-controlled evaluations, fixed-query scaling, and blinded annotation auditing systematically characterize retrieval performance. Across three training seeds, place-supervised SigLIP2 T2I Recall@1 decreases from 19.69% on source images to 12.31% on independent targets under P0. For fixed P1 queries, expanding the gallery from 500 to 8,425 places reduces Recall@1 from 14.67% to 2.87%. A 240-description audit further reveals discrepancies between automated acceptance and factual evidence. These findings establish AcrossPlace as a reproducible evaluation standard for assessing transferable place identity across visual observations and geographic environments.

replace VibePrevisBench: Evaluating Agentic Coding for 4D Scene Generation in Blender

Authors: Yuzhe Chen, Muxi Diao, Lele Yang, Wenbin Xu, Xuyang Liu, Qile Su, Changyi Liu, Jiankang Chen, Kaiyu Jiang, Haoyu Zhang, Hongyi Fu, Jianxiong Wang, Tianke Zhang, Haonan Fan, Yingxin Li, Bingyi Li, Xu Wang, Xuanyu Zheng, Bin Wen, Tingting Gao, Jiale Cao

Abstract: Controllable real-world video generation models can condition on a rendered graybox proxy video, which fixes layout, motion and camera. Coding agents can build such videos by generating a 4D scene in Blender, yet no benchmark checks both these rendered proxy videos and the complex, multi-shot scenes behind them. We introduce VibePrevisBench, a benchmark for 4D scene generation: an agent turns a brief into an executable 4D scene and renders its proxy video. Its 242 tasks span four input kinds, including scripts, photos, continuations, and reference videos. Each delivery is scored along two evaluation dimensions. Rule-based Scene Check re-runs the code and compares the exported 4D scene with hidden ground truth facts in nine dimensions. VLM-based Screen Check asks a VLM judge whether facts that pass the rules are recognizable in the proxy video. We analyze the evaluation results and trajectories of 11 frontier coding agents and identify three key observations. First, multimodal understanding separates the agents, especially in understanding space and camera motion. Second, all agents seem to already master basic camera work and staging, and fail at challenging requests. Third, agents inspect their own renders but fail to correct their errors. Agents thus need better visual understanding of space and camera, explicit checks for challenging requests, and item-by-item verification of renders against the brief.

replace Pros4D: Modeling Evolving 4D Worlds in Latent Space for Dynamic Spatial Reasoning

Authors: Rongxin Gao, Dongxuan Liu, Chu Li, Chengyang Ji, Jialing Huang, Runyu Chen, Kairun Wen, Chenxin Li, Jingrui Ye, Haosong Zhen, Xinghao Ding, Yue Huang, Yuzhi Huang, Xiaotong Tu, Jingyan Jiang

Abstract: Dynamic spatial reasoning from monocular video enables vision-language models (VLMs) to understand how 3D environments evolve over time, supporting physical world understanding and embodied intelligence. Existing efforts primarily address observed-dynamics understanding, whereas agents in dynamic environments also require future-dynamics prediction beyond the observation boundary. Moreover, external geometric processing and textual descriptions can increase inference complexity or lose information when representing continuous dynamics. These limitations motivate a shared internal state that preserves observed dynamics and remains predictive beyond the input video. Accordingly, we present Pros4D, a framework for modeling evolving 4D worlds in latent space that unifies retrospective understanding of observed-dynamics with prospective reasoning for future-dynamics prediction. Specifically, Pros4D-Engine derives geometry-grounded supervision and constructs paired examples for both tasks, while Pros4D-Bench evaluates them across eight spatial task categories spanning dynamic cameras, objects, and scenes. At the model level, Pros4D compresses observed dynamics into latent observed states and autoregressively rolls them forward into latent future states. 4D Latent Alignment (4DLA) grounds these states through complementary visual-semantic and geometric supervision, while joint language supervision connects them to task reasoning. Experiments across benchmarks show consistent improvements over strong baselines in both capabilities.

replace Passable for Whom? Few-Shot Learning for Personal Calibration of Sidewalk Accessibility Perception for Human-Centered Autonomous Driving

Authors: Wesley Maia, Angel Martinez-Sanchez, Maitrayee Keskar, Ross Greer

Abstract: Whether a sidewalk is passable depends on who is trying to pass: people who use the same mobility aid disagree about the same image, so one label per scene, or per aid, is wrong for many of them. We introduce few-shot personal accessibility perception: from K answers of a person never seen before, predict their judgment of sidewalk scenes never seen before. PassCal separates a shared description of the scene from the person's interpretation of it. The description comes from the internal state of a frozen vision-language model (VLM) and from barrier type, severity and context properties predicted by a detector trained on 14.9k public audits; the interpretation, a calibration of the person's response style and a weighting of the properties, is fit from their answers. On 8,883 judgments in which each of 135 verified mobility-aid users rated the same 50 sidewalk scenes, with people and scenes both held out, eight answers lower the Brier score from 0.550 to 0.479. The same VLM prompted with those answers reaches 0.562, and the same calibration without the scene 0.521. Conformal risk control turns predictions into passable, blocked or "depends on you" statements with a bounded expected rate of wrong statements over people; at 5%, eight answers raise the share of decisive scenes from 27% to 42%. Audit-supervised properties improve all sixteen vision and VLM encoders we test and outperform attributes from a sidewalk navigation VLM.

replace ReactBench-AD: Do Driving VLAs and World-Action Models React to Imminent Hazards in Their Path?

Authors: Nikos Theodoridis, Reenu Mohandas, Sushil Sharma, Ciar\'an Hogan, Ganesh Sistu, Anthony Scanlan, Tim Brophy, Ciar\'an Eising

Abstract: Vision-Language-Action models (VLAs) and World-Action Models (WAMs) have emerged in autonomous driving with the promise of strong generalization to long-tail scenarios. Although these models achieve state-of-the-art results on autonomous driving benchmarks, most existing benchmarks are dominated by nominal scenarios and therefore cannot test this promise. To test one such long-tail capability that is critical for safety, we introduce ReactBench-AD, a benchmark that measures how driving models react to objects that appear in the ego vehicle's path. Built on real NAVSIM and PhysicalAI-Autonomous-Vehicles scenes, ReactBench-AD composites a crossing pedestrian, a standing pedestrian, or a stationary car consistently across all camera views and history frames. Each edited scene thus forms an exact, pixel-level counterfactual pair with its original, which allows us to isolate the causal effect of the inserted object on the predicted trajectory. Evaluating four VLAs and two WAMs, we find that they react weakly, often barely at all. Finally, we test how easily one of the evaluated VLAs, ReCogDrive, can be taught to brake hard for imminent hazards: a short fine-tuning stage on edited scenes substantially improves its reactions, with the learned behavior generalizing to unseen objects and fully real scenes, at minimal cost to nominal driving. These results call into question the out-of-the-box long-tail capabilities of these models, but suggest that they can be a strong foundation for acquiring them with little additional training, highlighting the value of real or synthetic data that demonstrates the long-tail behaviors nominal datasets rarely contain.

replace CiGS: Conduction in Gaussian Splats for Interior Temperature Field from Thermal Images

Authors: Liu Zhenyuan, Bharath Seshadri, Bailey Miller, George Kopanas, Ioannis Gkioulekas, Bernd Bickel

Abstract: We present a method for inferring the interior temperature distribution of a solid object from registered multi-view visible-spectrum and thermal images, even when the thermal views cover only part of its surface. We formulate steady-state heat conduction as a Laplace boundary value problem and solve it with a direct boundary integral equation discretized over a point cloud: a Gaussian reconstruction of the visible-spectrum images supplies the boundary geometry, and the thermal images supply the boundary data. We do not build surface or volume meshes at any stage in our method. Instead, we sample collocation points from rendered depth without the pixels at depth discontinuities, and use a density subtraction that keeps the interior evaluation accurate up to the surface. The whole chain is differentiable, and we use it to recover the pose and depth of heating pipes, insulation defects, and the shape of cracks from thermal images. We validate our method through experiments on boundary value problems with Dirichlet-only and mixed Dirichlet and Robin boundaries, using synthetic and real captured data on scenes with challenging topology. These experiments show that our method matches analytical solutions and has better accuracy than FEM solvers.

replace BlockAlign: Preference Optimization for Trajectory-Distilled Video Generation

Authors: Jingyuan Zhu, Biaolong Chen, Yangyang Qin, Shengyang Shen, Yaohua Wang, Weiming Bai, Miao Lu, Aixi Zhang

Abstract: Directly aligning trajectory-distilled video generators slashes rollout costs and optimizes the deployed few-step model without teacher fine-tuning or re-distillation. However, conventional flow-matching preference objectives assume single-head local velocity supervision, mismatching the finite-interval, block-average transition interface of trajectory-distilled generators such as Parallel Decoding Distillation (PDD) and Parametric Trajectory Distillation (PTD). Naively supervising individual unfused sub-interval velocity heads across all sub-steps rather than the fused block-average velocity at block starts (DirectAlign) induces a severe training--inference mismatch that degrades preference below the unaligned baseline. We introduce BlockAlign, an interface-consistent preference optimization framework that constructs states at valid block boundaries, queries the fused block-level velocity predictor, and supervises span-normalized transition residuals---seamlessly supporting DPO-, NFT-, and rollout-style objectives while preserving the native few-step sampler. We further build a unified post-training pipeline integrating offline pairs, reward-free semi-online continuations, and online rollouts with hierarchical preference selection and objective-aware block scheduling. Experiments on multi-reference video generation across MiniMax-H3 Ref2VA and an in-house model RefVid-V4 show that BlockAlign consistently outperforms unaligned distilled models and DirectAlign in structural plausibility, reference consistency, and visual quality. Notably, our approach unlocks PDD's 5x few-step rollout speedup for 3x faster end-to-end online training, achieving statistically significant human preference gains over PDD baselines (54.0% vs. 33.3% wins/losses, or 61.8% non-tie win rate on RefVid-V4; 51.3% vs. 34.7%, or 59.7% non-tie win rate on MiniMax-H3) and up to an 82.7% win rate against DirectAlign.

replace Signs of Wear: Evaluating Intelligent Speed Assistance Under Synthetic Adverse Conditions

Authors: Johannes Fabian Hahn, Mao Shan, Julie Stephany Berrio Perez

Abstract: Intelligent Speed Assistance (ISA) systems rely on cameras to recognize speed-limit signs, yet existing benchmarks underrepresent the weathered, dirty, and overgrown signs commonly encountered on rural roads. We use generative image editing to introduce different forms of wear into real street-level images of speed-limit signs. The resulting ISA-Wear dataset contains paired original and edited images. Guided by text instructions, a multimodal image-editing model adds snow, rust, vegetation, faded paint, or mud to the speed-limit sign in a street-level frame at three severity levels. Where appropriate, the surrounding scene is adapted as well, and the viewpoint is kept. Every edited frame has an unedited counterpart, and we only evaluate frames whose original is recognized correctly, so any failure can be attributed to the wear. Since the model alters the sign's geometry in 21\% of the edits, all edits are filtered by a geometric check and a manual review. Severity is measured as the LPIPS distance between the original and the edited sign plate. On 716 worn frames, we find that in detect-then-read pipelines, wear breaks detection before it breaks reading. The detector finds only 62\% of the worn signs, even though it finds every one of them in the original frames. Missed detections account for 78--87\% of failed reads, a pattern that a zero-shot detector shares. Given the annotated sign box, as in the crop-based benchmarks, a classifier still reads 78\% of the worn signs, but only 56\% when it has to rely on the detector

replace Beyond Family Labels: A Taxonomic Ornstein-Uhlenbeck Prior for Avian 3D Shape Recovery

Authors: Yunchan Jeon, SooGon Kim, Seungwook Kim, CheolWon Lee, Jongmin Lee

Abstract: Recovering 3D pose and shape of birds from single images is bottlenecked by the long tail of avian diversity: out of ~10,000 species, only a few dozen have 3D-labeled training data, and in-the-wild test sets routinely contain species never seen at training. Generalizing across clades is therefore a central problem. The current state of the art, AniMer+, mitigates it with a family-level contrastive loss that conveys only a binary same-family indicator per pair and discards the continuous structure of the species tree. We propose a taxonomic Ornstein-Uhlenbeck prior on the shape code of the AVES parametric bird mesh model: a multivariate Gaussian whose covariance decays exponentially with Linnaean rank distance, with a learnable selection strength and noise scale. The prior generalizes family contrastive into a continuous, generative form, exposing the graded cross- and within-family similarity that a binary indicator cannot, and brings continuous comparative phylogenetics in as a biology-grounded inductive bias for the long tail. Our method improves over AniMer+ on both metrics of the unseen-species CowBird benchmark and on CtrlAVES3D, while remaining comparable on seen-species CUB. A permutation ablation isolates taxonomic structure from generic Gaussian smoothing, and a rank-granularity ablation identifies cross-order distinctions (the structure beyond a family-vs-not indicator) as the dominant source of the unseen-species gain.

replace Exploring the link between predictive uncertainty and the sim-to-real gap in deep learning models utilizing synthetic data

Authors: Alexander Moriz, Lukas Krebs, Amon G\"oppert, Robert H. Schmitt

Abstract: Today, state-of-the-art methods for quality control (QC) are based on Machine Vision, combining deep learning (DL) models with camera-based inspection. Yet, the integration of such solutions in industrial applications is often impeded by data sparsity and trust in model reliability. While synthetic data offers a promising approach to generate the necessary data with perfect annotations, it poses risks related to performance degradation due to the possible occurrence of the sim-to-real gap. This study conducts experiments using the ensemble method to estimate the predictive uncertainty of DL models trained on synthetic data. The results show that predictive uncertainty increases under the sim-to-real domain shift. However, the observed performance drop is dominated by a systematic sim-to-real bias rather than by ensemble disagreement.These findings contribute to the effective utilization of synthetic data in industrial settings while highlighting the importance of risk assessment for the deployment of DL solutions for QC tasks.

replace Decoding Without a Decoder: Direct Pixel Prediction from Diffusion Transformers

Authors: Hansheng Chen, Shengqu Cai, Jan Ackermann, Rayhan Zirvi, Gordon Wetzstein, Xinchen Yan, Leonidas Guibas

Abstract: Latent diffusion models rely on a decoder to map denoised latents to pixels, adding complexity and inference cost. We introduce Decoding Without a Decoder (DWD), which lets a finetuned diffusion transformer produce final pixels directly through a linear head. Unlike previous methods, where the denoising output leads to final pixels, DWD separates the two roles: the original denoising head continues to drive sampling, then the new DWD head predicts the pixels at the final step. We extend pretrained latent models with DWD efficiently by finetuning only the last few transformer blocks, training the DWD head with pixel quality losses while anchoring the denoising head to the pretrained model to preserve the sample trajectory. Across latent image and video generators, DWD removes the decoder while maintaining or even improving pixel quality. On Wan video generation, it visibly reduces detail distortion and flickering, lowering warping error by 23%. Remarkably, the same separation also improves pixel diffusion models that do not even use a decoder: a final-step DWD head produces substantially better texture detail in text-to-image generation, and improves ImageNet FID from 1.55 to 1.43, a new state of the art among pixel flow models.

replace MAVE: Reconstructing Editable 3D Blockouts from Film Shots

Authors: Yifan Chang, Jian Yang, Jiangnan Shao, Xiaofeng Wang, Zhaoxin Fan, Renjing Xu, Ming Lu, Xingang Wang, Zheng Zhu

Abstract: Animated 3D blockouts support previsualization and provide structural conditions for video generation, but manually reconstructing them from reference videos is costly. This limits both their use by creators and the production of paired training data. We introduce MAVEBench, a benchmark of 50 film shots and 416 keyframes for evaluating camera motion, spatial layout, and character blocking. We propose MAVE, a video-to-blockout framework combining Metric observation, Agentic modeling, and evidence-based VErification. It first recovers cameras, human motion, and geometric evidence in a shared metric coordinate system. An agent then constructs an editable environment under constraints from these observations, while programmatic checks report inconsistencies to guide refinement. The outputs are editable Blender scenes and frame-aligned blockout videos. On MAVEBench, MAVE improves overall scores by 27.5-41.1 points across three agents over their corresponding vanilla baselines. With DeepSeek-1017, it achieves a score of 73.2 at an average API cost of only USD 0.054 per shot, outperforming the strongest vanilla baseline while reducing total reconstruction time by 53%. We also construct MAVEData-3K, a dataset of 3,000 blockout-video pairs for model training, and will release it publicly. Creators can change viewpoints and character paths, then render the edited scenes to condition video generation. Code is available at https://github.com/yfChang-cv/MAVE.

replace Beyond the Camera: A Tri-Modal Driving World Model for Future RGB, Thermal, and LiDAR Forecasting in Adverse Weather

Authors: Faysal Mehrab Chowdhury, Md. Mahfuzur Rahman, Ahmed Rafi Hasan, Kishor Datta Gupta, Mohd Ariful Haque, Savannah Shannon

Abstract: Driving world models forecast what a vehicle's sensors will record next. Most forecast camera video and some add LiDAR, but thermal cameras, which still see in darkness and some adverse weather, are rarely a forecast target, and to our knowledge no world model forecasts all three sensors. We study joint forecasting of RGB, thermal, and LiDAR on real adverse-weather sequences of the DSERT-RoLL dataset. We post-train a pretrained video world model for each sensor and represent LiDAR as a depth video aligned with the RGB camera, with densification and encoding chosen by what the model's video tokenizer can reconstruct. A tri-sensor model sees all three sensors as one video. We score LiDAR in meters against measured returns and compare against persistence baselines passed through the same tokenizer as the model. With this matching, an apparent loss of the thermal model to persistence in fog disappears. On all 58 validation sequences in six weather conditions, post-trained RGB and thermal forecasts beat compression-matched persistence. Joint training lowers the LiDAR error at 2s by 2.1m at a small cost in camera fidelity, and only the tri-sensor model's LiDAR forecasts beat persistence in mean error. Blanking one sensor degrades the forecasts of the others more in snow than in fog.

replace TANGO: Real-World Unregistered RGB-Guided Hyperspectral Super-Resolution with Two-Camera Acquisitions

Authors: Matteo Kolyszko, Marco Buzzelli, Simone Bianco, Raimondo Schettini

Abstract: Unregistered hyperspectral image super-resolution aims to recover a high-resolution HSI from a low-resolution hyperspectral observation and an unregistered high-resolution RGB reference. Although recent methods have begun to address the geometric misalignment between the two modalities, their evaluation is still constrained by the lack of a large-scale public benchmark based on real two-camera RGB-HSI acquisitions with a high-resolution HSI reference. To address this gap, we introduce DUET, the first large-scale public dataset for unregistered RGB-guided hyperspectral super-resolution, comprising 403 indoor and outdoor scenes captured with two separate RGB and hyperspectral cameras. We further propose TANGO (Two-camera AligNment with Gated Occlusions), which first compensates for the dominant cross-camera displacement through end-to-end global alignment and then refines the remaining inconsistencies with a coarse-to-fine local feature alignment. A learned gating mechanism further attenuates the RGB guidance where the two views cannot be consistently matched. Experimental results show that TANGO achieves state-of-the-art performance on both DUET and REAL across all evaluated scale factors, outperforming the strongest competitor on DUET by up to 2.29 dB in PSNR while requiring 2.5$\times$ fewer FLOPs. The code and dataset will be available at https://github.com/FagiolottoConSombrero/TANGO-DUET

URLs: https://github.com/FagiolottoConSombrero/TANGO-DUET

replace Visual Harness Scales General VLMs to Medical Images

Authors: Shaowen Wan, Hanqi Jiang, Zihao Wu, Junhao Chen, Weihang You, Ruiyu Yan, Bowen Chen, Tianming Liu, Lin Zhao

Abstract: We show that an appropriate visual harness can substantially improve the performance of general vision-language models (VLMs) across diverse medical imaging tasks. We introduce VERA, a medical visual harness that equips general VLMs with interactive visual tools and systematic diagnostic evidence management. With VERA, general VLMs interactively explore medical images and progressively acquire diagnostic evidence, which is preserved in a lossless memory for subsequent retrieval and use. After submitting an initial answer, the VLM receives an assessment of potential omissions and the reliability of identified findings. This assessment guides further image inspection and measurement, followed by answer revision in an iterative agentic loop until sufficient evidence is obtained. Across 11 tasks and four imaging modalities, VERA improves performance on all tasks with Qwen3.8-27B, with notable gains in spleen measurement (63.3% to 98.3%), renal search (57.5% to 71.2%), and pulmonary nodule detection (0.0% to 28.2%). High-capability API-based VLMs achieve even higher performance with VERA. For example, GPT-6.1 Sol improves pulmonary nodule detection from 10.8% to 69.2% with VERA, demonstrating the complementary benefits of stronger model capabilities and an effective medical visual harness. Our results highlight the potential of visual harnesses as a new paradigm for scaling general VLMs to medical imaging tasks.

replace Code World Model 2.0: Towards Infinite Controllable Video Generation

Authors: Ruoyu Wang, Hao Zhong, Yanming Yang, Jiahao Tian, Lin Liu, Biao Gong, Guoshan Lu, Yiding Tian, Chunhua Shen, Zhenzhong Lan, Chi Zhang

Abstract: Visual proxies decouple explicit world-state simulation from photorealistic video synthesis, offering a controllable interface for both offline clip creation and real-time interactive streaming. Yet a practical renderer must address two challenges. Upstream proxies vary from coarse layouts to fine articulated geometry depending on the interaction, and streaming inference must operate in a few causal steps without accumulating drift across self-generated history. We present Invoker-Render, pairing a bidirectional creation model with a causal four-step streaming model under a shared conditioning interface over structural proxies, an appearance reference, and language. Trained on an aligned approximately 200-hour multi-granularity corpus, Invoker-Render honors fine geometric constraints when supplied while synthesizing plausible local motion under coarse proxies, enabling runtime selection of input detail. To bridge bidirectional creation and real-time streaming without compounding step compression and rollout drift, we introduce Progressive Forcing (ProForcing). ProForcing first trains a causal predictor and distills it into a four-step renderer using histories derived from training videos. Training then shifts to continuous self-generated rollouts. A pipelined serving stack supports efficient streaming inference. Experiments show versatile control across proxy families, granularities, and camera paths, consistent gains over recent streaming baselines, and stable 30-minute racing rollouts that preserve scene and subject appearance.

replace Check Your Blind Spots: Decision-Guided Perception by Probing the Planner with Hypothetical Objects

Authors: Hoang Pham Cong, Long D. Nguyen, Ho Viet Duc Luong, Nguyen Tran Minh Nhat, Binh P. Nguyen

Abstract: Not every perception error changes the planner's action. Yet adaptive-resolution perception chooses where to spend high-resolution computation from image cues, which can neither reliably point to an object that low resolution missed nor tell which objects matter to the planner. We observe that the planner can say where an object would matter before anything is seen: inserting a hypothetical object at a location tests whether its action would change. Decision-Guided Speculative Perception (DGSP) treats the low-resolution scene and its action as a draft and probes the planner in this way to obtain a decision-sensitivity field, the locations where an unseen object would change the action. Only this field and the support, the detections on which the current action rests, are verified at high resolution, repeatedly until the action is stable. For two planner classes, we prove that DGSP returns exactly the action of whole-frame high-resolution perception if crop detections match whole-frame ones and the crops cover the field, conditions that real images meet approximately. On three driving datasets, it reproduces this action on about 98% of frames with about a quarter of the pixels. At the pixel cost of uniform downscaling, it repairs more of the draft's disagreements with this action, brakes falsely less often, and recovers many more of the decision-relevant objects that the draft misses.

replace M3OPD-Reasoner: An Open Recipe for Post-Training Multimodal Reasoning with Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation

Authors: Nguyen Quang Trung, Hoang Phan, Shuo Sun, Longyin Zhang, AiTi Aw

Abstract: Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation (MOPD) enables a single student to absorb the skills of several domain teachers by learning from its own generated responses. We present Multimodal Model Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation Reasoner (M3OPD-Reasoner), a fully open recipe for post-training vision-language models (VLMs) over three reasoning domains: image STEM, image perception, and video. We curate two datasets, M3OPD-SFT-1.35M for supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and M3OPD-RL-193K for reinforcement learning (RL) with verifiable rewards. On these data, we compare post-training recipes that start from Qwen3.5-9B: training one model on the mixed data of all domains (MixSFT and MixRL), and training one teacher per domain with Group Sequence Policy Optimization (GSPO), directly or after an SFT warm-up, and distilling the teachers into one student with MOPD. We evaluate on 21 image STEM, image perception, and video reasoning benchmarks after we decontaminate the training data against them. The comparison establishes MOPD of teachers trained with GSPO directly from Qwen3.5-9B as the best recipe: the student closely matches each teacher on its own domain and is on average above the mixed-training baselines on the same prompts. Since MOPD already merges the domain capabilities that MixRL would otherwise have to learn, its student is a stronger starting point for RL, and we ask whether the same data can carry the model further from there: we add MixRL on top of the MOPD student, on the same prompts and with the same settings, which, to our knowledge, no prior VLM recipe has studied. The same prompts still improve the MOPD student, with no collapse, and give our main model, M3OPD-Reasoner-9B. MOPD and MixRL can therefore work as consecutive stages that get more out of the same data. We release all data, the training code, and the checkpoints of every stage.

replace Any2Building: Unifying Point Clouds and Images for Building Reconstruction via Shared Spatial Queries

Authors: Hongye Hou, Zhuoxiao Li, Liu Xiangyu, Tengxi Wang, Jing Ou, Dongli Wu, Shuai Zhang, Tongyan Hua, Wufan Zhao

Abstract: Abstracting structured buildings from limited observations is essential for 3D urban modeling and digital twins. Partial point clouds and overhead images of the same building provide incomplete 3D surface measurements and visual cues to its footprint, roof geometry, and rooftop structures, respectively. This motivates a unified framework that aligns geometric and visual cues in 3D and reconstructs buildings from either or both modalities. Building on this connection, we present Any2Building, a unified model for Level of Detail 2 (LoD2) building reconstruction from partial point clouds, overhead images, or both, with optional coarse geometric priors. At its core is an occupancy-grounded shared spatial queries representation that organizes available observations within a common 3D grid. Supervised by complete building surface voxels, these queries connect observational evidence to target geometry, enabling a shared generator to learn structural priors across tasks and providing spatial guidance for recovering missing structure. A trained voxel conditional surface-generation and low-poly remeshing pipeline then converts the predicted structure into compact LoD2 building models. To support this framework, we introduce BuildingWorld-Image, comprising approximately one million paired samples of partial point clouds, overhead images, and LoD2 building models across 18 cities. Experiments on multiple benchmarks demonstrate that Any2Building outperforms existing methods in geometric reconstruction accuracy across unimodal and multimodal point-cloud completion, single-image building generation, and prior-assisted generation, while maintaining detailed and robust recovery when point cloud input modality is degraded.

replace Multimodal Masked Diffusion Autoencoder for Modeling Impaired Driving

Authors: John Gideon, Anshul Gupta, Megan Applegate-Kenton, Kayli Battel, Todd J Rowell, Patricio Reyes Gomez, Laporsha Dees, Emily Sumner, Avinash Balachandran, Guy Rosman

Abstract: Human cognitive state (e.g., distraction, stress), gaze, and behavior interact with the driving environment in subtle ways that AI could use to assist drivers, but ground-truth labels for human cognitive state levels are costly to obtain at scale. replace-cross Mask-guided Spectral-wise Transformer for Efficient Hyperspectral Image Reconstruction

Authors: Yuanhao Cai, Jing Lin, Xiaowan Hu, Haoqian Wang, Xin Yuan, Yulun Zhang, Radu Timofte, Luc Van Gool

Abstract: Hyperspectral image (HSI) reconstruction aims to recover the 3D spatial-spectral signal from a 2D measurement in the coded aperture snapshot spectral imaging (CASSI) system. The HSI representations are highly similar and correlated across the spectral dimension. Modeling the inter-spectra interactions is beneficial for HSI reconstruction. However, existing CNN-based methods show limitations in capturing spectral-wise similarity and long-range dependencies. Besides, the HSI information is modulated by a coded aperture (physical mask) in CASSI. Nonetheless, current algorithms have not fully explored the guidance effect of the mask for HSI restoration. In this paper, we propose a novel framework, Mask-guided Spectral-wise Transformer (MST), for HSI reconstruction. Specifically, we present a Spectral-wise Multi-head Self-Attention (S-MSA) that treats each spectral feature as a token and calculates self-attention along the spectral dimension. In addition, we customize a Mask-guided Mechanism (MM) that directs S-MSA to pay attention to spatial regions with high-fidelity spectral representations. Extensive experiments show that our MST significantly outperforms state-of-the-art (SOTA) methods on simulation and real HSI datasets while requiring dramatically cheaper computational and memory costs. Code and pre-trained models are available at https://github.com/caiyuanhao1998/MST/ . An online Gradio demo is available on HuggingFace at https://huggingface.co/spaces/CaiYuanhao/MST

replace-cross HDNet: High-resolution Dual-domain Learning for Spectral Compressive Imaging

Authors: Xiaowan Hu, Yuanhao Cai, Jing Lin, Haoqian Wang, Xin Yuan, Yulun Zhang, Radu Timofte, Luc Van Gool

Abstract: The rapid development of deep learning provides a better solution for the end-to-end reconstruction of hyperspectral image (HSI). However, existing learning-based methods have two major defects. Firstly, networks with self-attention usually sacrifice internal resolution to balance model performance against complexity, losing fine-grained high-resolution (HR) features. Secondly, even if the optimization focusing on spatial-spectral domain learning (SDL) converges to the ideal solution, there is still a significant visual difference between the reconstructed HSI and the truth. Therefore, we propose a high-resolution dual-domain learning network (HDNet) for HSI reconstruction. On the one hand, the proposed HR spatial-spectral attention module with its efficient feature fusion provides continuous and fine pixel-level features. On the other hand, frequency domain learning (FDL) is introduced for HSI reconstruction to narrow the frequency domain discrepancy. Dynamic FDL supervision forces the model to reconstruct fine-grained frequencies and compensate for excessive smoothing and distortion caused by pixel-level losses. The HR pixel-level attention and frequency-level refinement in our HDNet mutually promote HSI perceptual quality. Extensive quantitative and qualitative evaluation experiments show that our method achieves SOTA performance on simulated and real HSI datasets. Code and models will be released at https://github.com/caiyuanhao1998/MST . An online Gradio demo is available on HuggingFace at https://huggingface.co/spaces/CaiYuanhao/MST

replace-cross Degradation-Aware Unfolding Half-Shuffle Transformer for Spectral Compressive Imaging

Authors: Yuanhao Cai, Jing Lin, Haoqian Wang, Xin Yuan, Henghui Ding, Yulun Zhang, Radu Timofte, Luc Van Gool

Abstract: In coded aperture snapshot spectral compressive imaging (CASSI) systems, hyperspectral image (HSI) reconstruction methods are employed to recover the spatial-spectral signal from a compressed measurement. Among these algorithms, deep unfolding methods demonstrate promising performance but suffer from two issues. Firstly, they do not estimate the degradation patterns and ill-posedness degree from the highly related CASSI to guide the iterative learning. Secondly, they are mainly CNN-based, showing limitations in capturing long-range dependencies. In this paper, we propose a principled Degradation-Aware Unfolding Framework (DAUF) that estimates parameters from the compressed image and physical mask, and then uses these parameters to control each iteration. Moreover, we customize a novel Half-Shuffle Transformer (HST) that simultaneously captures local contents and non-local dependencies. By plugging HST into DAUF, we establish the first Transformer-based deep unfolding method, Degradation-Aware Unfolding Half-Shuffle Transformer (DAUHST), for HSI reconstruction. Experiments show that DAUHST significantly surpasses state-of-the-art methods while requiring cheaper computational and memory costs. Code and models will be released at https://github.com/caiyuanhao1998/MST . An online Gradio demo is available at https://huggingface.co/spaces/CaiYuanhao/MST

Abstract: X-ray, known for its ability to reveal internal structures of objects, is expected to provide richer information for 3D reconstruction than visible light. replace-cross Radiative Gaussian Splatting for Efficient X-ray Novel View Synthesis

Authors: Yuanhao Cai, Yixun Liang, Jiahao Wang, Angtian Wang, Yulun Zhang, Xiaokang Yang, Zongwei Zhou, Alan Yuille

Abstract: X-ray is widely applied for transmission imaging due to its stronger penetration than natural light. When rendering novel view X-ray projections, existing methods mainly based on NeRF suffer from long training time and slow inference speed. In this paper, we propose a 3D Gaussian splatting-based method, namely X-Gaussian, for X-ray 3D reconstruction. Firstly, we redesign a radiative Gaussian point cloud model inspired by the isotropic nature of X-ray imaging. Our model excludes the influence of view direction when learning to predict the radiation intensity of 3D points. Based on this model, we develop a Differentiable Radiative Rasterization (DRR) with CUDA implementation. Secondly, we formulate a principled Radiative Gaussian Volume Querying (RGVQ) method that defines a continuous attenuation field from the original radiative point attributes and enables direct CT volume reconstruction through CUDA-based parallel querying. Thirdly, we customize an Angle-pose Cuboid Uniform Initialization (ACUI) strategy that directly uses the parameters of the X-ray scanner to compute the camera information and then uniformly samples point positions within a cuboid enclosing the scanned object. On novel view synthesis, X-Gaussian outperforms SOTA methods by 6.5 dB while enjoying less than 15% training time and over 73x inference speed. On CT reconstruction, X-Gaussian outperforms SOTA methods by 0.40 dB and 4.34 dB on synthetic and real scenes, while achieving 4.30x faster inference of the learned CT volume. Code, models, and datasets are at https://github.com/caiyuanhao1998/X-Gaussian . More video and interactive visualizations are presented on our project page at https://caiyuanhao1998.github.io/project/X-Gaussian . We also built an online Gradio demo page on HuggingFace at https://huggingface.co/spaces/CaiYuanhao/X-Gaussian .

URLs: https://github.com/caiyuanhao1998/X-Gaussian, https://caiyuanhao1998.github.io/project/X-Gaussian, https://huggingface.co/spaces/CaiYuanhao/X-Gaussian

replace-cross Survival of the Fittest Perturbations: Uncertainty-Aware Evolution of Adversarial Distributions for Robust Generalization

Authors: Junhao Dong, Zicheng Yu, Xinghua Qu, Z. Jane Wang, Yew-Soon Ong

Abstract: Despite deep learning's broad successes across diverse scenarios, its vulnerability to adversarial examples impedes deployment, making adversarial training a leading approach to improve robustness. However, pointwise adversarial training maps each clean sample to a single adversary, overlooking surrounding adversarial variability and the associated shifts in feature statistics. To address these issues, we propose Evolutionary Uncertainty-Aware Distributional Adversarial Training (EvoUAD-AT). Specifically, we design an evolutionary adversarial uncertainty modeling module that maintains an instance-wise search distribution over adversarial feature statistics around an adversarially generated anchor. Candidates are evaluated under the current model, selected by their threat to a benign reference, and used to adapt the distribution; strategy parameters are retained across epochs. To avoid anchoring on misclassified clean samples, we refine the alignment reference and match benign and adversarial distributions. Introspective gradient matching further regularizes input gradients without external supervision. Our analysis characterizes the elite fitness surrogate and gives a conditional certificate for latent adversarial features. Extensive evaluations across multiple benchmarks demonstrate that our approach consistently outperforms existing state-of-the-art methods in both clean accuracy and robustness, with thorough analyses confirming the effectiveness of each proposed component.

replace-cross MARBLE: An Agentic Benchmark for Spatial Reasoning and Visual Abstraction

Authors: Yulun Jiang, Na\"el Ouerghemi, Yekun Chai, Maria Brbi\'c, Michael Moor

Abstract: A central challenge for multimodal language models (MLLMs) in real-world tasks is to abstract visual observations into structured spatial states and reason over how those states change under actions over long horizons. Existing multimodal benchmarks often focus on static question answering, where answers can be directly extracted from images, or assume symbolic states are available and bypass the visual-to-spatial abstraction process. We present MARBLE, a diagnostic benchmark for spatially grounded multimodal reasoning and planning across three agent environments: CUBE, MAZE, and BLOX. MARBLE spans 2D and 3D spatial puzzles under geometric constraints, dynamic layouts, and spatial transformations, with difficulty systematically controlled along two axes: observation format and planning horizon. Extensive evaluations show that frontier MLLMs struggle on MARBLE, with the strongest model GPT-5.4 achieving only a 7.3% average success rate across the three environments in the hardest setting. Pairwise ablations on the open-weight models identify visual-to-spatial abstraction as the major bottleneck: when models must infer spatial states from images instead of receiving human-annotated states, success drops from 50.7% to 3.7% for Gemma-4-31B and from 87.9% to 6.5% for Kimi-K2.6. The results indicate that models often fail to construct precise spatial states from visual observations before planning, a task that is straightforward for humans. Overall, MARBLE reveals the gap in reliable visual-to-spatial abstraction for frontier MLLMs and provides a controlled testbed for measuring progress in spatially grounded multimodal reasoning and planning.

replace-cross AERR-Nav: Adaptive Exploration-Recovery-Reminiscing Strategy for Zero-Shot Object Navigation

Authors: Jingzhi Huang, Junkai Huang, Haoyang Yang, Haoang Li, Yi Wang

Abstract: Zero-Shot Object Navigation (ZSON) in unknown multi-floor environments presents a significant challenge. Recent methods, mostly based on semantic value greedy waypoint selection, spatial topology-enhanced memory, and Multimodal Large Language Model (MLLM) as a decision-making framework, have led to improvements. However, these architectures struggle to balance exploration and exploitation for ZSON when encountering unseen environments, especially in multi-floor settings, such as robots getting stuck at narrow intersections, endlessly wandering, or failing to find stair entrances. To overcome these challenges, we propose AERR-Nav, a Zero-Shot Object Navigation framework that dynamically adjusts its state based on the robot's environment. Specifically, AERR-Nav has the following two key advantages: (1) An Adaptive Exploration-Recovery-Reminiscing Strategy, enables robots to dynamically transition between three states, facilitating specialized responses to diverse navigation scenarios. (2) An Adaptive Exploration State featuring Fast and Slow-Thinking modes helps robots better balance exploration, exploitation, and higher-level reasoning based on evolving environmental information. Extensive experiments on the HM3D and MP3D benchmarks demonstrate that our AERR-Nav achieves state-of-the-art performance among zero-shot methods. Comprehensive ablation studies further validate the efficacy of our proposed strategy and modules.

replace-cross What Should Visual Place Recognition Be Invariant To? Rethinking Condition Invariance in Visual Place Recognition

Authors: Beomsu Kim, Minwoo Jung, Giseop Kim

Abstract: Condition invariance in Visual Place Recognition (VPR) is typically evaluated by matching queries from one condition against a database from another condition. This tests the ability to recognize the same place when the query's condition differs from the database's condition. However, to map large environments, a database can be built from images collected by multiple robots or across multiple traversals under different conditions. In this setting, condition blindness---the ability to remain invariant to condition differences among database images---becomes important, but standard cross-condition evaluation does not provide a controlled assessment of this ability. Therefore, we introduce Distractor-Augmented Recall (DAR) to measure condition blindness in a controlled manner. Across eleven methods and six datasets, we find that differences in condition blindness can be larger than Recall@1 alone would suggest. To improve the condition blindness that DAR reveals, we compare three methods that make VPR descriptors more invariant to condition changes through post-processing. Among the three methods, we demonstrate INLP on a database built from multiple traversals under different conditions, showing that it can improve the performance of existing VPR methods.

replace-cross Neoadjuvant chemotherapy response prediction using pretreatment diffusion and contrast-enhanced magnetic resonance imaging with clinical variables

Authors: Pablo Garc\'ia Marcos, Paula Puerta Gonz\'alez, Guillermo Lorenzo, H\'ector G\'omez, Covadonga del Camino, Ad\'an Rodr\'iguez, Ignacio Pel\'aez, Angel Rio-Alvarez, V\'ictor M. Gonz\'alez

Abstract: Prediction of pathological complete response before neoadjuvant chemotherapy may facilitate more tailored therapeutic planning for breast cancer patients. This work proposes a deep-learning model for pretreatment data only, combining apparent diffusion coefficient maps, dynamic contrast-enhanced magnetic resonance imaging, and clinical variables. The study uses the public ACRIN 6698/I-SPY2 multicenter dataset. The architecture employs EfficientNet-B0 pretrained encoders for image feature extraction and late fusion with clinical information. Multiple clinical variables were evaluated, including age, race, histological type, HR/HER2 subtype, SBR grade, and maximum diameter. Only HR/HER2 subtype improved the average area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUC) and was retained in the final model. Using stratified five-fold cross-validation, standalone apparent diffusion coefficient maps achieved a mean AUC of 0.79, whereas dynamic contrast-enhanced magnetic resonance imaging achieved 0.74. Adding HR/HER2 subtype improved performance to 0.83 and 0.81, respectively. The final configuration, using both imaging modalities and HR/HER2 subtype, achieved an AUC of 0.86. These results support pretreatment multimodal learning for response prediction, although external validation is required before clinical use.

replace-cross On Agentic Manipulation Policies

Authors: Chen Chen, Omid Bagherieh, Naveen Nikamanth Appiah Balaji, Jesse T. Gonzalez, Negin Heravi, Fahad Islam, Xueting Li, Jean-Baptiste Regli, Kejia Ren, Petr \v{S}vec, David J. Yoon, Matthias M\"uller, Vladlen Koltun

Abstract: Recent work on robot manipulation has focused on specialist policies trained on demonstrations. We show that generalist AI models can control manipulators more reliably than specialist policies such as vision-language-action models (VLAs) and world-action models (WAMs). We systematically analyze agentic manipulation policies (AMPs), in which a generalist AI model commands the manipulator directly, adapting its actions as the task unfolds. We present AMP1, a reference harness that runs unchanged across single-arm, bimanual, and mobile manipulation. AMP1 achieves state-of-the-art results on the RoboLab-120 and CaP-Gym benchmarks, with no demonstrations or task-specific training. On RoboLab-120, AMP1 reaches an 85.6% success rate zero-shot, compared to 49.2% and 42.9% for leading VLA and WAM policies. The agent succeeds zero-shot in 104 of 108 real-world trials spanning twelve tasks and three robot instances, reducing failure rates by 20x and 15x relative to strong VLA and WAM baselines. The basic harness supplies no grasping, pushing, or placing skills; the agent develops its own strategies, such as searching for hidden objects, using objects as tools, and calibrating its cameras. With the addition of a persistent memory store, the agent can also learn from experience: an augmented harness with a memory representation that is carried across episodes lowers the failure rate on RoboDojo bimanual manipulation tasks by 4.6x relative to zero-shot deployment. Our analysis suggests that AMPs are substantially more general and robust than specialist manipulation policies, providing a promising foundation for robot manipulation.

replace-cross Measuring and Attributing Evidence Sensitivity in Medical Vision-Language Models via Symmetric Counterfactual Controls

Authors: Bhaskar Gurram

Abstract: Medical vision-language models (VLMs) asked closed clinical questions ("Is there a polyp?") can score well by relying on the question rather than the image, and current hallucination benchmarks cannot separate true visual discrimination from editing shortcuts or shifts in the answer threshold. We introduce a Counterfactual Evidence Framework that pairs each lesion erasure with a shape- and tool-matched control edit elsewhere in the image, adds same-site ring controls, restored and partial erasures, degraded views and natural negatives, tests on held-out inpainting tools, separates discrimination (the Evidence Sensitivity Gap, ESG, and AUROC) from the answer threshold, and checks the labels with blinded physician readers and hidden-state probes. Across 507 main-test cases (1,115 views), nine frontier VLMs claim 41.1% to 61.6% of erased lesions (ESG 0.269 to 0.470), whereas MedScope-4B, a Qwen3.5-4B agent trained on automatically built counterfactual trajectories, claims 9.6% (ESG 0.758; p <= 0.001 in all 27 primary comparisons). Physician readers support automated erasure in colonoscopy, where frontier models still claim 45% of erased lesions against 1% for MedScope-4B, but call a finding present on 54.7% of pooled erased reads across all sources, which bounds what unverified erasure can certify. Across 12 pre-registered training or system arms and 270 decision policies screened under a rule fixed in advance, every apparent utility gain comes from shifting the threshold rather than improving held-out discrimination, and probes point to perception as the remaining limit. We also report a 0/48 pneumothorax failure on an external cohort; retraining with pneumothorax patients in every stage finds 53% of pneumothoraxes on new patients without changing the main-test results.

replace-cross TetraCoder: Adaptive Tetrahedral Encoding for Neural Fields

Authors: Daniele Della Pietra, Antonella Rech, Nicola Conci, Nicola Garau

Abstract: Implicit and explicit representations of geometry are central in computer graphics applications and come with their pros and cons. With surface sampling being faster on explicit representations and spatial queries being faster on implicit representations, finding efficient conversions between them is a highly desirable property. We present TetraCoder, a single resolution hash-free adaptive tetrahedral encoding that links an explicit tetrahedral spatial encoder with an underlying implicit signed distance field (SDF). The resulting hybrid implicit-explicit space is continuous piecewise affine (CPWA) by design, allowing to obtain an exact correspondence between the implicit SDF and the explicit analytic mesh. TetraCoder's design comes with additional perks, in particular: (a) it scales better than multi-resolution encoding methods due to its single resolution optimizable grid, (b) unlike hash-based methods, it is not affected by collisions, (c) it enables fast tetrahedral ray-tracing, geometry and appearance editing, directly through its adaptive tetrahedral scaffold. We demonstrate the versatility of TetraCoder by showing an improvement over state-of-the-art methods on multiple tasks: 2D/3D fitting from images and point clouds, 3D novel view synthesis, 3D reconstruction, and geometry/appearance editing.

replace-cross Gamer: A Generalist Vision-Language-Action Foundation Model for Long-Horizon Real-Time Game Agent

Authors: Run Luo, Kuan Zhang, Qi Deng, Yukun Chen, Zhihao Yang, Yufei Zhang, Renrong Hu, Yi Zhang, Guiyu Ma, Tianyu Fan, Longze Chen, Hao Sun, Jiaming Li, Jiaming Yang, Xiaohao Liu, Tao Tan, Minzheng Wang, Hangtian Jia, Shuai Yuan, Lvdi Wang, Xiao Liu, Chao Ma, Yuting Zou, Yinhe Zheng, Xiaobo Xia, Dawei Jiang, Shaoqing Ren

Abstract: We introduce Gamer, a generalist agent framework built for long-horizon tasks in complex interactive environments. Its end-to-end architecture combines perception, memory management, and multi-step reasoning. A shared training and deployment pipeline supports interaction with both conventional 2D graphical interfaces and complex 3D simulated worlds. We first continue pre-training on a dataset constructed from tens of thousands of hours of device-level keyboard and mouse interaction. These trajectories span simulated games and embodied environments, allowing the model to learn environmental dynamics across both physical and virtual domains. Sparse-thinking instruction tuning then substantially reduces redundant computation while preserving the reasoning needed for complex decisions. We train the policy for long-horizon interaction with a stable multi-turn reinforcement learning method that addresses finite context windows, accumulated rollout errors, and sparse rewards. For real-time interaction, a cloud-edge inference engine serves numerous simulated game environments concurrently, supports unbounded-context execution, and keeps online inference latency low. Across multiple challenging 3D open-world games, Gamer demonstrates solid generalization and operational efficiency comparable to skilled players. It also achieves competitive results on a range of conventional 2D game benchmarks. These results suggest that even repetitive keyboard and mouse trajectories can support substantial gains from scaling data and learning algorithms. Training on these trajectories helps Gamer learn complex skills more steadily in environments with both physical and digital properties. This approach offers a path toward generalist agents that can interact across digital and physical worlds.

replace-cross Heave-Ho: Decentralized Long-Horizon Multi-Humanoid Collaboration

Authors: Ting-Hsuan Chen, Siheng Zhao, Han Jiang, Qi Pan, Louison Lu, Jiaheng Luo, Henghui Bao, Yiyu Chen, Quan Nguyen, Yue Wang

Abstract: Collaboration promises to extend humanoid capabilities beyond what individual robots can achieve. However, realizing this potential requires coordinated whole-body interactions, coherent long-horizon planning, and precise synchronization across robots. We introduce Heave-Ho, a decentralized framework for long-horizon collaborative multi-humanoid loco-manipulation. At the low level, we develop a multi-agent post-training framework that adapts a humanoid foundation tracking model to physically coupled interactions between humanoids, enabling coordinated decentralized control from local observations alone. At the high level, we train a vision-language planner and design a speech-based leader-follower coordination mechanism to synchronize decentralized execution without network communication. Trained with two identical humanoids, our system scales to teams of up to ten robots and generalizes to heterogeneous teams without retraining. To benchmark these capabilities, we introduce HH-Bench, comprising 12 tasks involving teams of 2 to 10 humanoids, including heterogeneous teams. Systematic evaluations show that Heave-Ho consistently outperforms baseline methods, while real-world experiments demonstrate robust decentralized collaboration across challenging tasks.

replace-cross CADMAGEDDON: High-Fidelity CAD Reconstruction with Local Inference

Authors: Oleg Pavlov, Nikita Gavrilov, Soslan Kabisov, Gennadii Savrasov, Andrey Kuznetsov, Anton Konushin, Dmitrii Zhemchuzhnikov

Abstract: CAD practitioners often work with proprietary parts that they are unwilling to share with external services. For individuals and small companies, bringing this work in-house also requires computational resources they can afford. We introduce CADMAGEDDON, an open-source agent that reconstructs editable CAD from meshes and can run locally on single-GPU systems and current-generation laptops. The method decomposes this complex task into a chain of simple visual decisions about what to construct and what to correct. Geometric tools support precise construction, allowing the agent to focus on visual reasoning. Using the mid-sized Qwen3.8-27B model, CADMAGEDDON outperforms prior approaches, including systems based on frontier models, in reconstruction quality on all the main benchmarks. CADMAGEDDON integrates with FreeCAD, bringing its reconstructions into practitioners' existing CAD workflows. Code: https://github.com/kulibinai/cadmageddon

URLs: https://github.com/kulibinai/cadmageddon

replace-cross MillBill: A Machining Agent with Geometric Feedback

Authors: Gennadii Savrasov, Artem Slavin, Soslan Kabisov, Andrey Kuznetsov, Anton Konushin, Dmitrii Zhemchuzhnikov

Abstract: Turning a CAD model into a machining program requires both process knowledge and geometric precision. Language models can generate such workflows by writing geometry and toolpath code for each part, but this makes numerical execution part of the model's task. We introduce MillBill, an agent that separates machining decisions from their geometric execution and verification. Given a boundary-representation part, stock, and material, a locally hosted, mid-sized open model proposes operations and cutting parameters for indexed three-axis machining. A geometric harness generates toolpaths, simulates material removal, and returns feedback for plan refinement. Setup planning accounts for what the available cutters can physically reach, rather than visibility alone. We evaluate the resulting programs by the fraction of the target surface area within the specified tolerance after simulated machining. With Qwen3.8-27B, MillBill outperforms frontier-model approaches that construct their own geometry and toolpath workflows on the evaluated machining tasks. Code: https://github.com/kulibinai/millbill

URLs: https://github.com/kulibinai/millbill

replace-cross RFW-S: Everyday Deformable Objects with Independent Simulation Verification

Authors: Jiarui Guo, Saining Zhang, Jiaying Wan, Jiawei Weng, Junhao Chen, Rui Zhang, Yuanbo Lyu, Zhiheng Lee, Peishuo Li, Jingrun Du, Weiqing Xiao, Weiyi Lin, Zenan Meng, Hu Wei, Lin Qu, Zongzheng Zhang, Hao Zhao, Chaojian Li

Abstract: Large-scale robotic simulation requires diverse deformable objects with executable physical properties and realistic interaction behaviors. However, existing 3D asset collections primarily emphasize visual diversity, while scalable physical construction and verification remain challenging. We introduce RFW-S, a large-scale dataset of 83,365 simulation-verified assets spanning 485 everyday categories across five structural families: soft solids, cloth sheets, shells, garments, and bags. We develop an automated pipeline integrating reference-guided 3D generation, structure-aware physical modeling, and LLM-assisted parameter estimation and calibration. To ensure physical reliability, we introduce an independent simulation verification framework that evaluates object behaviors under interactions distinct from those used for parameter calibration. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of independent verification and characterize deformable responses under different physical configurations. We further evaluate robotic grasping in terms of both task success and object integrity, and demonstrate the physical realism of our simulated objects through sim-to-real replay of fragile-object manipulation. RFW-S provides a scalable foundation for physically grounded simulation and embodied robotic learning.
